<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For $`N=2^d`$ cards, we prove that an absolute number of coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle brings the full permutation law to total-variation distance tending to zero from uniform. The mixing time therefore has optimal order $`\Theta(\log N)`$ in physical shuffles.

- **claim-routing-density-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fac-main)): There are absolute constants $\eta,c,C>0$ such that, with $a=1/1000$, $$\log\mathbb E_{y\sim u_{N,k}}R_x(y)^{1+a} \le Ck(k/N)^\eta \qquad(1\le k\le N),$$ uniformly over every starting injection $x$. For every sufficiently large dyadic $N$ and every nontrivial irreducible representation, $$T_N(\lambda)=0\quad\hbox{or}\quad \\|T_N(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le D_\lambda^{-c}.$$ Consequently an absolute number of forward sweeps has worst-start total-variation distance from uniform tending to zero as $d\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Bounds a density moment of the ordered partial-permutation routing law of a reflected sweep, gives a dimension-power contraction of one sweep on nontrivial irreducibles, and concludes that a fixed number of sweeps mixes.
- **claim-sign-annihilation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-annihilation)): The sign representation is annihilated by every nonempty fair layer. For $n\ge2$, every irreducible of $S_n$ indexed by a shape with more than $n/2$ rows is annihilated by a sweep.
  - Explanation: The sign representation is killed by any nonempty fair layer, and shapes with too many rows are killed by a full sweep.
- **claim-finite-size-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finitegap)): For every fixed dyadic $n\ge2$, $\\|T_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}<1$ on every nontrivial irreducible.
  - Explanation: At each fixed dyadic size the sweep has operator norm strictly below one on every nontrivial irreducible.
- **claim-support-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-support)): For every integer $t\ge0$, $$\\|q_d^{*t}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\ge1-2^{tn/2}/n!.$$ Consequently $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\ge\lceil(2/n)\log_2(3n!/4)\rceil=2d-O(1)$.
  - Explanation: Counting the random bits used limits the number of reachable permutations, which forces a lower bound on the mixing time of order twice the dimension.
- **claim-safe-sweep-powers** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-schatten)): For integers $r\ge s\ge1$ and any matrix $T$, setting $Q=T^*T$, $$\\|T^r\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le\\|Q\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{r-s}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Q^s.$$
  - Explanation: Hilbert-Schmidt norm of a power of an arbitrary, possibly nonnormal, matrix is controlled by the operator norm and a trace moment of T-star T.
- **claim-degree-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degrees)): Uniformly in $n$, $\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}D_\lambda^{-1}$ is bounded, and $$\sum_{\lambda\ne(n),(1^n)}D_\lambda^{-1}\longrightarrow0.$$ If $\ell=n-\max(\lambda_1,\lambda'_1)$, then $$\log D_\lambda\ge(\log2)\sqrt\ell/2, \qquad \sup_n\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}D_\lambda^{-32}<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Inverse-dimension sums over partitions stay bounded, tend to zero away from the extreme shapes, and individual dimensions grow with the distance from a row or column shape.
- **claim-truncation-repeated-copies** (bound, [source](#stmt-net-truncation)): Let a finite group act transitively on a set of size $M$, and let $Q$ be the symmetric Markov kernel induced on its permutation module by a probability measure on that group. Suppose $Q=A+E$ entrywise, where $A,E\ge0$, every entry of $A$ is at most $B/M$, and the row and column sums of $E$ are at most $p$. If an irreducible representation occurs with multiplicity $m>0$, then $$\\|Q(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le \sqrt{B/m}+p.$$ It is not necessary that $A$ preserve the irreducible subspaces.
  - Explanation: Splitting a symmetric Markov kernel into a bounded-entry part and a small-mass part converts multiplicity of an irreducible into operator-norm decay.
- **claim-butterfly-contacts** (bound, [source](#stmt-n-butterfly-contacts)): Any $h$ distinct routed paths through a butterfly that updates each of $d$ binary coordinates once have at most $h\log_2h/2$ shared switches in total, with $0\log_2 0=0$. Their contact graph has maximum degree at most $d$. Counting both path endpoints at each shared switch gives at most $h\log_2h$ contacts.
  - Explanation: Deterministic bound on the total number of shared switches among distinct routed paths in a butterfly, plus a degree bound on the contact graph.
- **claim-cycle-factorial-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-fac-cycle-factorial)): On a finite set take a permutation given by independent butterflies of block size $n=2^r$ in parallel, composed before and after with arbitrary fixed permutations (we allow identifications of the sets at each stage). Fix a subset of size at most $k$, and count cycles lying entirely inside it, with $F_j$ the number of length $j$. For nonnegative integers $a_j$ (finitely supported) and $A=\sum a_j$, $$\mathbb E\prod_j(F_j)_{a_j}\ \le\ (k/\sqrt n)^A\prod_j j^{-a_j/2}. \label{fac:e3}$$ Here $(F)_a=F(F-1)\cdots(F-a+1)$ and $(F)_0=1$.
  - Explanation: Factorial moments of cycle counts of a permutation built from fresh butterflies between fixed maps decay with the cycle lengths.
- **claim-palindromic-density-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-fac-density)): Let $N=2^d$, $1\le k\le N$, and $a=1/1000$. For a kernel $Q$ on ordered $k$-injections define $$M(Q,k)=\sup_{x\in\mathcal X_{N,k}}\mathbb E_{y\sim u_{N,k}}\big[(N)_kQ(x,y)\big]^{1+a}.$$ There are absolute $C,\eta>0$ with the following properties; below $M$ denotes $M(Q,k)$ for the kernel in question.* - *for $K_N$, $\log M\le C k(k/N)^{\eta}$, for some absolute $C<\infty$ and $\eta>0$;* - *consider the modified palindrome obtained by replacing the central subnetworks of size $S=2^L$ by independent uniform permutations, except in at most $b$ specified such blocks where they are replaced by identity. For every $\xi>0$, there is $L_0$ such that for each fixed $L\ge L_0$ there are $\epsilon>0$ and $d_0\ge L$ for which $M(Q,N)\le e^{\xi N}$ whenever $d\ge d_0$ and $bS/N\le\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: Density moments of the reflected sweep kernel on injections, and of modified networks with central blocks replaced by uniform permutations, are controlled at the stated exponents.
- **claim-high-height-cycle-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-net-hightail)): There are absolute $b,\delta>0$ and an integer $J_0\ge1$ such that, uniformly in the tracked input injection of size $r$ and for every integer $J\ge J_0$, $$\begin{aligned} \log_2\mathbb E2^{b\sum_{j\ge J}C_j}&\le O(r2^{-bJ}),\label{net:highmgf}\\ \log_2\mathbb E2^{b\sum_jC_j}&\le O(r(r/n)^\delta).\label{net:sparsemgf} \end{aligned}$$ The first bound also holds with expectation conditional on all input-side switches and all output switches of heights at most $s$, for every integer $0\le s\le d$ with $2s<J$, uniformly in the fixed switch values and with the same absolute implied constant.
  - Explanation: Exponential moments of cycle counts at high heights decay in the height cutoff, and the total over all heights has a power saving in the tracked density.
- **claim-dense-truncation** (bound, [source](#stmt-net-densetruncation)): For every $\rho_0\in(0,1]$ and $\epsilon>0$, there are $c>0$ and $n_0$ such that, for every dyadic $n\ge n_0$ and every integer $r$ with $\rho=r/n\in[\rho_0,1]$, the density exponent <a href="#net:exponent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="net:exponent">[net:exponent]</a> exceeds $$r\left[H(\rho)+\frac{-\rho-(1-\rho)\ln(1-\rho)}{\rho\ln2}+\epsilon\right]$$ with probability at most $\exp(-cr)$, uniformly in the input injection. The constants depend only on $\rho_0,\epsilon$, and the expression is interpreted continuously at $\rho=1$.
  - Explanation: At fixed tracked density, the density exponent exceeds an explicit threshold with exponentially small probability.
- **claim-certificate-network-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-net-main)): There is an absolute $c>0$ such that for every sufficiently large dyadic $n$ and every nontrivial irreducible of dimension $D$ not annihilated by a pair layer, $$\\|T_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le D^{-c}.$$ Consequently a fixed absolute number of sweeps has total-variation distance tending to zero.
  - Explanation: A sweep contracts every surviving nontrivial irreducible by a fixed power of its dimension, so a fixed number of sweeps mixes.
- **claim-strong-density-both-orientations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-strong-density)): There are absolute constants $p\in(1,2]$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for every $N=2^d$, every coordinate order, every $0\le l\le N$, either choice of $H_N$, and every $x\in\mathcal X_{N,l}$, $$\label{n:strong-injection-density} \frac1{(N)_l}\sum_{y\in\mathcal X_{N,l}} \big((N)_lH_N(x,y)\big)^p\le e^{Cl}.$$ After increasing $C$, the same constants give $$\label{n:strong-density-power-normalization} N^{-l}\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^{dl}} \big(N^lH_N(x,y)\big)^p\le e^{Cl},$$ where the kernel is extended by zero on tuples with repeated positions. Both conclusions hold with rows replaced by columns.
  - Explanation: An exponential-in-tracked-cards moment bound on the routing density holds for every injection size, coordinate order and both positive orientations.
- **claim-strong-pointwise-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-strong-smoothing)): There are an absolute integer $u\ge1$ and an absolute $C<\infty$ such that, for every $N=2^d$, every coordinate order, every $0\le l\le N$, every $x,y\in\mathcal X_{N,l}$, and either $H_N=T_N^*T_N$ or $H_N=T_NT_N^*$, $$\label{n:strong-smoothing-eq} H_N^u(x,y)\le\frac{e^{Cl}}{(N)_l}.$$ In particular, with unnormalized regular trace on $S_N$, $$\label{n:strong-regular-smoothing} \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{reg}}\|T_N\|^{2u}\le e^{CN}.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed power of either positive orientation is pointwise bounded by an exponential factor over the number of injections, giving a regular trace bound.
- **claim-strong-tail-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-n-strong-tail)): There are absolute constants $a>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for every dyadic $N$, every coordinate order, and every partition $\lambda=(N-k,\beta)\vdash N$, with $\beta=(\lambda_2,\lambda_3,\ldots)\vdash k$, $$\label{n:strong-tail-eq} \\|T_N(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \le e^{Ck}D_\beta^{-a}.$$ Here $D_\beta=\dim V_\beta$, including $D_\varnothing=1$. One may take $a=1/p'=(p-1)/p$ for the exponent in Theorem <a href="#n:strong-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="n:strong-density">14</a>.
  - Explanation: The squared sweep norm on a shape is bounded by an exponential in the level times a negative power of the lower-diagram dimension.
- **claim-uniform-contact-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-n-coherent-contact)): There is an absolute constant $C_*>0$ such that for every dyadic $n=2^d\ge2$, every $1\le k<n$, and every partition $\lambda=(n-k,\beta)\vdash n$, $$\label{n:coherent-contact-sharp} \\|T_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \le \min\left\{1,\left(\frac{C_*dk}{n}\right)^{k/2}\right\}.$$ For $k=1$ this operator is zero. In particular, with an absolute constant $C$ independent of $n,k,\lambda$, $$\label{rec:coherent-contact-bound} \\|T_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \le C^k(1+d)^{Ck}(k/n)^{k/2}.$$ The same statements hold with the coordinate order reversed.
  - Explanation: Cancellation of isolated tracked paths gives a contraction of the sweep on each level with constants uniform in the number of tracked cards.
- **remark-root-count-forest** (bound, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): For comparison with counting roots among physical positions, the right side of <a href="#n:contact-weighted-forest-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="n:contact-weighted-forest-count">[n:contact-weighted-forest-count]</a> equals $$4^k(k/n)^k \sum_{1\le j\le k/2}\frac{n^j}{j!}(2d)^{k-j}.$$ For $j\le n/2$, the bound $$\log\frac{n^j}{(n)_j} =\sum_{r=0}^{j-1}-\log(1-r/n) \le\sum_{r=0}^{j-1}\frac{r}{n-r}\le j$$ shows that $n^j/j!\le e^j\binom nj$. Thus we also obtain the root-count bound $$\label{rec:coherent-forest-count} \Pr\{\mathcal O\text{ has no isolated vertex}\mid\text{return randomness}\} \le(k/n)^k\sum_{1\le j\le k/2}(4e)^k\binom nj(2d)^{k-j}.$$ The weighted version identifies all types and occupation factors explicitly; both forms retain the same number $k-j$ of contact costs.
  - Explanation: Restates the forest-count bound with roots counted among physical positions.
- **claim-sparse-level-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-n-coherent-contact-sparse-saving)): There are absolute constants $C,d_0>0$ such that for every dyadic $n=2^d$ and $\lambda=(n-k,\beta)\vdash n$ satisfying $1\le k<n$ and $\log(n/k)\ge d^{3/4}$, $$\\|T_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \le e^{Ck}(k/n)^{k/4}.$$ For $d\ge d_0$, the factor $e^{Ck}$ can be omitted.
  - Explanation: In the sparse regime the squared sweep norm obtains an exponential saving in the level.
- **claim-sparse-dimension-power** (bound, [source](#stmt-n-coherent-sparse-consequence)): There are absolute $c>0$ and $d_0$ such that, for every $N=2^d$ with $d\ge d_0$ and every partition $\lambda=(N-k,\beta)\vdash N$ satisfying $1\le k<N$ and $\log(N/k)\ge d^{3/4}$, $$\\|T_N(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\le D_\lambda^{-c}.$$
  - Explanation: Combining the sparse contact bound with the tail-dimension bound gives a negative power of the full dimension in the sparse regime.
- **claim-cycle-density-casimir-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cas-main)): There are absolute constants $a,C>0$ such that for every dyadic $n$, every $1\le k\le n$, and every $x\in X_{n,k}$, $$\\|R_x\\|_{L^{65/64}(u_{n,k})}\le e^{C h_n(k)}.$$ For every nontrivial partition $\lambda\vdash n$, put $k=n-\lambda_1$. Then $$\\|K_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le e^{-a h_n(k)},\qquad D_\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}K_n(\lambda)^{65}\le e^{C h_n(k)}.$$ Consequently there is an absolute integer $l$ for which $\\|\mu_n^{*l}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\to0$ as $n\to\infty$ through powers of two. Here $U_{S_n}$ is the uniform permutation law and total variation includes the factor $1/2$. The same bound holds from every deterministic initial permutation. When $n=2^d$, these $l$ sweeps take $ld$ physical shuffles.
  - Explanation: A density bound at a slightly higher exponent, exponential contraction on the Casimir scale, and a trace moment bound together give mixing after an absolute number of sweeps.
- **claim-tensor-swap-recursion** (bound, [source](#stmt-cas-swap-recursion)): For a dyadic $m$ and an integer $q\ge1$, let $D$ be the action of $K_m$ on $(\mathbb C^q)^{\otimes m}$. For $S\subseteq[m]$, let $A_S=\operatorname{Tr}_{S^c}D$, where the partial trace is unnormalized. Then $$Z_{2m}(q)=2^{-m}\sum_{S\subseteq[m]}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}A_S^2, \qquad Z_{2m}(q)\le Z_m(q)Z_m(1+(q-1)/2).$$ The scalar function $Z_m(x)$ in the second formula is defined for every real $x\ge1$, including a nonintegral argument.
  - Explanation: The cycle generating trace at double size is expressed through partial traces over swapped legs and bounded by a product of two child traces with a smaller alphabet.
- **claim-split-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-cas-split-moments)): There are absolute constants $t_0,t_1>0$ and $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every dyadic $n\ge2$ and every nontrivial $\lambda\vdash n$, with $k=n-\lambda_1$ and $c=c_\lambda$, $$\left\\|\mathbb E_s e^{t\Delta/n}\right\\|_\lambda\le C_0 \quad\text{at }t=t_0\log(e n/k),\qquad \left\\|\mathbb E_s e^{t_1\mathcal R^2/(nc)}\right\\|_\lambda\le C_0, \label{cas:e10}$$ The expectations are over the independent fair signs defining the transversal split, and both norms are operator norms on $V_\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Under a random transversal split, the Casimir sum defect and the squared child imbalance have bounded exponential operator moments.
- **claim-fixed-first-row-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-cas-fixed-level)): For every fixed integer $k\ge2$ there are constants $C_k,n_k$ such that, for every dyadic $n=2^d\ge n_k$ and every $\lambda\vdash n$ with $\lambda_1=n-k$, $$\\|K_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le C_k(d/n)^{k/2}.$$ For $k=1$, $K_n(\lambda)=0$ at every admissible size.
  - Explanation: For a fixed small deficit the kernel norm is bounded by a power of the coordinate count over the size.
- **claim-harmonic-cycle-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-main-harmonic-cycle)): There are absolute constants $c,C,\zeta>0$, a number $\delta\in(0,1)$, and an integer $p_0$ with the following properties. For every dyadic $n$ and $\lambda=(n-k,\beta)\vdash n$ with $k\ge1$, $$\begin{aligned} W_n(\lambda)&\le e^{-c\Lambda_n(k)},\label{rec:level-contraction}\\ W_n(\lambda)&\le e^{C\Lambda_n(k)}f_\beta^{-\zeta}. \label{rec:tail-contraction} \end{aligned}$$ Independently, for every $1\le k\le n$, let $X_{n,k}$ be the ordered injective $k$-tuples of positions, with uniform probability measure, and let $\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)=(n)_k\Pr_{K_n}(x\mapsto y)$ be its kernel density. Then $$\begin{aligned} \sup_x\mathbb E_y\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)^{1+\delta} &\le e^{C\Lambda_n(k)},\label{rec:tuple-moment}\\ \operatorname{tr}_{X_{n,k}}K_n^{p_0} &\le e^{C\Lambda_n(k)}.\label{rec:tuple-flat-trace} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, after changing absolute constants, $$\begin{aligned} \\|T_n\|_{V_\lambda}\\|_{\mathrm{op}} &\le \exp[-c\{\Lambda_n(k)+\log f_\beta\}], \label{rec:combined-tail-norm}\\ W_n(\lambda)&\le e^{-c_1\Lambda_n(k)}D_\lambda^{-c_2} \label{rec:combined-dimension-norm} \end{aligned}$$ for absolute $c_1,c_2>0$.
  - Explanation: Level contraction, tail-dimension contraction, a tuple density moment and a flat tuple trace bound are obtained from harmonic restriction.
- **claim-removal-splitting** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-removal-splitting)): For the component law above, $a$ is a mixture of $t+\operatorname{Bin}(k-2t,1/2)$. In particular $$\label{rec:split-tail} \Pr(\|a-k/2\|\ge z)\le2e^{-2z^2/k},\qquad \mathbb Ee^{u(a-b)^2/k}\le1+Cu\quad(0\le u\le1/4).$$ For every fixed $\eta>0$ and integer $l\ge1$, $$\label{rec:loss-binomial-general} \mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf1_{\{m-a-r\ge\eta m\}} {a-r\choose l}\right] \le(C_\eta\sqrt{k/n})^l,$$ and the same statement holds in the other half. Thus, with $\alpha=1/8$ and $$L_1=(a-r)\mathbf1_{\{a\le3m/4,\ r<\alpha m\}},\qquad L_2=(b-s)\mathbf1_{\{b\le3m/4,\ s<\alpha m\}},$$ we have $\mathbb E{L_i\choose l}\le(C\sqrt{k/n})^l$ and $$\label{rec:loss-mgf} \mathbb Ee^{u\log(en/k)L_i}\le1+Cu \quad(0\le u\le u_0)$$ for an absolute $u_0>0$. A second useful form, with $\alpha=1/16$, is $$\label{rec:loss-tail} \Pr\big((a-r)\mathbf1_{\{r<\alpha m\}}\ge x\big) \le C e^{-cx\log(en/k)}\qquad(x\in\mathbb Z_{\ge1}).$$
  - Explanation: Label occupancy of the two halves is a binomial mixture and the loss of representation level under slot removal has exponentially small tails.
- **claim-entropy-deficit-cycles** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-entropy-deficit)): For any fixed input tuple of $k$ distinct positions and any law $Q$ on the raw switches, the actual tracked cycle counts satisfy $$\label{rec:cycle-tilted-entropy} \mathbb E_Q\sum_j C_j^*\le C\Lambda_n(k)+C D(Q\Vert\mathrm{fair}),$$ with an absolute constant $C$.
  - Explanation: Under an arbitrary law on switches the expected tracked cycle total is charged to the relative entropy against the fair law.
- **claim-adaptive-alphabet-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-adaptive-main)): There are absolute positive constants $c,c',C$ such that for every dyadic $n$ and every partition $\lambda=(n-r,\rho)\vdash n$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|F_n\|_{V_\lambda}\\|_{\mathrm{op}}&\le e^{-c h_n(r)}, \label{rec:generator-level-main}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{V_\lambda}F_n^4&\le \exp\{-c'\log D_\lambda+C h_n(r)\}. \label{rec:adaptive-trace-main} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently $\\|T_n\|_{V_\lambda}\\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ is at most $\exp[-c_0\{\log D_\lambda+h_n(r)\}]$ for an absolute $c_0>0$.
  - Explanation: Compressed generators give a level contraction and a fourth trace moment with a negative dimension power, combined into a dimension-power norm bound.
- **claim-compressed-mgfs** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-compressed-mgfs)): Let $1\le r\le n-1$ and let $P$ act on the ordered injective $r$-tuple module by simultaneously switching matched positions. Put $p_*=r/n$. For absolute $t_0,t_1,v_0>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|Pe^{tT}P\\|&\le e^{Cp_*\|t\|},&&\|t\|\le t_0, \label{rec:T-small-mgf}\\ \\|Pe^{\pm t_1\log(1/p_*)T}P\\|&\le1+o(1),&&p_*\longrightarrow0, \label{rec:T-log-mgf}\\ \\|Pe^{vJ^2/r}P\\|&\le1+O(v),&&0\le v\le v_0. \label{rec:J-square-mgf} \end{aligned}$$ The estimates are uniform in $n,r$.
  - Explanation: Compressed transposition difference operators on injective tuples have controlled exponential moments.
- **claim-alphabet-angle** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-alphabet-angle)): For compatible parent and child types, $$\\|WPE\\|^2\le \prod_{\mathrm{row}}G_q(\mu)G_q(\nu)\, e^{-(\mathcal L-\mathcal L_0-\mathcal L_1)/2}.$$
  - Explanation: The operator angle between compatible parent and child projections is bounded using joint concavity of operator geometric means.
- **claim-alphabet-cycle-inflation** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-alphabet-cycle-inflation)): Let $s$ be the total number of named labels and $\sigma_i=\log\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}F_m^4$ on the child induced modules. If $m\ge2$, $$\label{rec:alphabet-pair-trace} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(PY^2P)^2\le g_0g_1e^{\sigma_0+\sigma_1}\, 2^{\gamma s/2}\prod_{\mathrm{regular}} \left(2^{-b}\binom b{b_0}\right), \qquad\gamma=\frac{\log_2 3}{2}<1.$$ For $m=1$ the inflation $2^{\gamma s/2}$ may be replaced by $2^{s/2}$.
  - Explanation: The fourth trace at a split is bounded by child traces times a cycle inflation from named labels.
- **claim-core-power-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-core-power-main)): There is an absolute $c>0$ such that $\\|T_n\|_{V_\lambda}\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2^{-cF_\lambda}$ for every surviving nontrivial type.
  - Explanation: Charging core boxes gives a dimension-power norm bound for every surviving nontrivial type.
- **claim-cutoff-one-node** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-cutoff-one-node)): Let $B_0,B_1$ be positive operators in the group algebra of $S_m$, let $B=H(B_0\otimes B_1)H$, and fix $\lambda\vdash2m$ and $p\ge2$. Let $\mathcal I_\lambda$ consist of the pairs $(\mu,\theta)$ with $c_{\mu\theta}^\lambda>0$, and write $N_\lambda=\|\mathcal I_\lambda\|$. For such a pair use $$M_\mu=B_0(\mu)^{p/2},\quad M_\theta=B_1(\theta)^{p/2},\qquad t_\mu=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}B_0(\mu)^p,\quad t_\theta=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}B_1(\theta)^p$$ and form $T_{\mu\theta}$ from their coefficient-square densities above. Pairs with a zero trace are omitted. Then $$\label{rec:cutoff-one-node-bound} D_\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}B(\lambda)^p \le N_\lambda^2 \max_{(\mu,\theta)\in\mathcal I_\lambda} (D_\mu t_\mu)(D_\theta t_\theta)T_{\mu\theta}.$$ If all selected traces vanish, the left side is zero.
  - Explanation: The dimension-weighted trace at a parent type is bounded by child pair quantities times a weighted cycle factor.
- **claim-cutoff-midpoint** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-cutoff-midpoint)): There is an absolute $c_1>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large dyadic $n$ and all surviving nontrivial $\lambda\vdash n$ with $k\ge n^{1/100}$, $$\label{rec:cutoff-low-bound} \max\left\{\\|T_nP_{\rm lo}^D\|_{V_\lambda}\\|, \\|P_{\rm lo}^CT_n\|_{V_\lambda}\\|\right\} \le2^{-c_1F_\lambda}.$$ The two projections refer to different product subgroups; no commutation between them is asserted.
  - Explanation: Projections onto midpoint product types of small total dimension have exponentially small overlap with the sweep.

```json
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite group act transitively on a set of size $M$, and let $Q$ be the symmetric Markov kernel induced on its permutation module by a probability measure on that group.       },

        "exact_quote": "On a finite set take a permutation given by independent butterflies of block size $n=2^r$ in parallel, composed before and after with arbitrary fixed permutations (we allow identifications of the sets at each stage). Fix a subset of size at most $k$, and count cycles lying entirely inside it, with $F_j$ the number of length $j$.         "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-238.6"

        "exact_quote": "Let $N=2^d$, $1\\le k\\le N$, and $a=1/1000$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-net-hightail",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute $b,\\delta>0$ and an integer $J_0\\ge1$ such that, uniformly in the tracked input injection of size $r$ and for every integer $J\\ge J_0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\log_2\\mathbb E2^{b\\sum_{j\\ge J}C_j}&\\le O(r2^{-bJ}),\\label{net:highmgf}\\\\\n \\log_2\\mathbb E2^{b\\sum_jC_j}&\\le O(r(r/n)^\\delta).\\label{net:sparsemgf}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first bound also holds with expectation conditional on all input-side switches and all output switches of heights at most $s$, for every integer $0\\le s\\le d$ with $2s<J$, uniformly in the fixed switch values and with the same absolute implied constant.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-strong-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "There are an absolute integer $u\\ge1$ and an absolute $C<\\infty$ such that, for every $N=2^d$, every coordinate order, every $0\\le l\\le N$, every $x,y\\in\\mathcal X_{N,l}$, and either $H_N=T_N^*T_N$ or $H_N=T_NT_N^*$, $$\\label{n:strong-smoothing-eq}\n H_N^u(x,y)\\le\\frac{e^{Cl}}{(N)_l}.$$ In particular, with unnormalized regular trace on $S_N$, $$\\label{n:strong-regular-smoothing}\n \\operatorname{Tr}_{\\mathrm{reg}}|T_N|^{2u}\\le e^{CN}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C_*>0$ such that for every dyadic $n=2^d\\ge2$, every $1\\le k<n$, and every partition $\\lambda=(n-k,\\beta)\\vdash n$, $$\\label{n:coherent-contact-sharp}\n \\|T_n(\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}^2\n \\le \\min\\left\\{1,\\left(\\frac{C_*dk}{n}\\right)^{k/2}\\right\\}.$$ For $k=1$ this operator is zero.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
        "exact_quote": "For comparison with counting roots among physical positions, the right side of <a href=\"#n:contact-weighted-forest-count\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"n:contact-weighted-forest-count\">[n:contact-weighted-forest-count]</a> equals $$4^k(k/n)^k\n \\sum_{1\\le j\\le k/2}\\frac{n^j}{j!}(2d)^{k-j}.$$ For $j\\le n/2$, the bound $$\\log\\frac{n^j}{(n)_j}\n =\\sum_{r=0}^{j-1}-\\log(1-r/n)\n \\le\\sum_{r=0}^{j-1}\\frac{r}{n-r}\\le j$$ shows that $n^j/j!\\le e^j\\binom nj$.         "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $a,C>0$ such that for every dyadic $n$, every $1\\le k\\le n$, and every $x\\in X_{n,k}$, $$\\|R_x\\|_{L^{65/64}(u_{n,k})}\\le e^{C h_n(k)}.$$ For every nontrivial partition $\\lambda\\vdash n$, put $k=n-\\lambda_1$. Then $$\\|K_n(\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le e^{-a h_n(k)},\\qquad\n D_\\lambda\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}K_n(\\lambda)^{65}\\le e^{C h_n(k)}.$$ Consequently there is an absolute integer $l$ for which $\\|\\mu_n^{*l}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\to0$ as $n\\to\\infty$ through powers of two. Here $U_{S_n}$ is the uniform permutation law and total variation includes the factor $1/2$. The same bound holds from every deterministic initial permutation. When $n=2^d$, these $l$ sweeps take $ld$ physical shuffles.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cas-swap-recursion",
        "exact_quote": "For a dyadic $m$ and an integer $q\\ge1$, let $D$ be the action of $K_m$ on $(\\mathbb C^q)^{\\otimes m}$. For $S\\subseteq[m]$, let $A_S=\\operatorname{Tr}_{S^c}D$, where the partial trace is unnormalized. Then $$Z_{2m}(q)=2^{-m}\\sum_{S\\subseteq[m]}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}A_S^2,\n \\qquad\n Z_{2m}(q)\\le Z_m(q)Z_m(1+(q-1)/2).$$ The scalar function $Z_m(x)$ in the second formula is defined for every real $x\\ge1$, including a nonintegral argument.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cas-swap-recursion",
          "exact_quote": "For a dyadic $m$ and an integer $q\\ge1$, let $D$ be the action of $K_m$ on $(\\mathbb C^q)^{\\otimes m}$.           "source_id": "PM-238.6"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cas-split-moments",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $t_0,t_1>0$ and $C_0<\\infty$ such that, for every dyadic $n\\ge2$ and every nontrivial $\\lambda\\vdash n$, with $k=n-\\lambda_1$ and $c=c_\\lambda$, $$\\left\\|\\mathbb E_s e^{t\\Delta/n}\\right\\|_\\lambda\\le C_0\n\\quad\\text{at }t=t_0\\log(e n/k),\\qquad\n\\left\\|\\mathbb E_s e^{t_1\\mathcal R^2/(nc)}\\right\\|_\\lambda\\le C_0,\n\\label{cas:e10}$$ The expectations are over the independent fair signs defining the transversal split, and both norms are operator norms on $V_\\lambda$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cas-split-moments",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $t_0,t_1>0$ and $C_0<\\infty$ such that, for every dyadic $n\\ge2$ and every nontrivial $\\lambda\\vdash n$, with $k=n-\\lambda_1$ and $c=c_\\lambda$, $$\\left\\|\\mathbb E_s e^{t\\Delta/n}\\right\\|_\\lambda\\le C_0\n\\quad\\text{at }t=t_0\\log(e n/k),\\qquad\n\\left\\|\\mathbb E_s e^{t_1\\mathcal R^2/(nc)}\\right\\|_\\lambda\\le C_0,\n\\label{cas:e10}$$ The expectations are over the independent fair signs defining the transversal split, and both norms are operator norms on $V_\\lambda$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $t_0,t_1>0$ and $C_0<\\infty$ such that, for every dyadic $n\\ge2$ and every nontrivial $\\lambda\\vdash n$, with $k=n-\\lambda_1$ and $c=c_\\lambda$, $$\\left\\|\\mathbb E_s e^{t\\Delta/n}\\right\\|_\\lambda\\le C_0\n\\quad\\text{at }t=t_0\\log(e n/k),\\qquad\n\\left\\|\\mathbb E_s e^{t_1\\mathcal R^2/(nc)}\\right\\|_\\lambda\\le C_0,\n\\label{cas:e10}$$ The expectations are over the independent fair signs defining the transversal split, and both norms are operator norms on $V_\\lambda$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cas-fixed-level",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed integer $k\\ge2$ there are constants $C_k,n_k$ such that, for every dyadic $n=2^d\\ge n_k$ and every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ with $\\lambda_1=n-k$, $$\\|K_n(\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le C_k(d/n)^{k/2}.$$ For $k=1$, $K_n(\\lambda)=0$ at every admissible size.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rec-main-harmonic-cycle",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c,C,\\zeta>0$, a number $\\delta\\in(0,1)$, and an integer $p_0$ with the following properties. For every dyadic $n$ and $\\lambda=(n-k,\\beta)\\vdash n$ with $k\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{-c\\Lambda_n(k)},\\label{rec:level-contraction}\\\\\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)}f_\\beta^{-\\zeta}.\n \\label{rec:tail-contraction}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Independently, for every $1\\le k\\le n$, let $X_{n,k}$ be the ordered injective $k$-tuples of positions, with uniform probability measure, and let $\\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)=(n)_k\\Pr_{K_n}(x\\mapsto y)$ be its kernel density. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_x\\mathbb E_y\\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)^{1+\\delta}\n   &\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)},\\label{rec:tuple-moment}\\\\\n \\operatorname{tr}_{X_{n,k}}K_n^{p_0}\n   &\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)}.\\label{rec:tuple-flat-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, after changing absolute constants, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|T_n|_{V_\\lambda}\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n   &\\le \\exp[-c\\{\\Lambda_n(k)+\\log f_\\beta\\}],\n       \\label{rec:combined-tail-norm}\\\\\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{-c_1\\Lambda_n(k)}D_\\lambda^{-c_2}\n       \\label{rec:combined-dimension-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for absolute $c_1,c_2>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-main-harmonic-cycle",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c,C,\\zeta>0$, a number $\\delta\\in(0,1)$, and an integer $p_0$ with the following properties. For every dyadic $n$ and $\\lambda=(n-k,\\beta)\\vdash n$ with $k\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{-c\\Lambda_n(k)},\\label{rec:level-contraction}\\\\\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)}f_\\beta^{-\\zeta}.\n \\label{rec:tail-contraction}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Independently, for every $1\\le k\\le n$, let $X_{n,k}$ be the ordered injective $k$-tuples of positions, with uniform probability measure, and let $\\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)=(n)_k\\Pr_{K_n}(x\\mapsto y)$ be its kernel density. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_x\\mathbb E_y\\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)^{1+\\delta}\n   &\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)},\\label{rec:tuple-moment}\\\\\n \\operatorname{tr}_{X_{n,k}}K_n^{p_0}\n   &\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)}.\\label{rec:tuple-flat-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, after changing absolute constants, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|T_n|_{V_\\lambda}\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n   &\\le \\exp[-c\\{\\Lambda_n(k)+\\log f_\\beta\\}],\n       \\label{rec:combined-tail-norm}\\\\\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{-c_1\\Lambda_n(k)}D_\\lambda^{-c_2}\n       \\label{rec:combined-dimension-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for absolute $c_1,c_2>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $c,C,\\zeta>0$, a number $\\delta\\in(0,1)$, and an integer $p_0$ with the following properties. For every dyadic $n$ and $\\lambda=(n-k,\\beta)\\vdash n$ with $k\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{-c\\Lambda_n(k)},\\label{rec:level-contraction}\\\\\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)}f_\\beta^{-\\zeta}.\n \\label{rec:tail-contraction}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Independently, for every $1\\le k\\le n$, let $X_{n,k}$ be the ordered injective $k$-tuples of positions, with uniform probability measure, and let $\\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)=(n)_k\\Pr_{K_n}(x\\mapsto y)$ be its kernel density. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_x\\mathbb E_y\\mathcal K_{n,k}(x,y)^{1+\\delta}\n   &\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)},\\label{rec:tuple-moment}\\\\\n \\operatorname{tr}_{X_{n,k}}K_n^{p_0}\n   &\\le e^{C\\Lambda_n(k)}.\\label{rec:tuple-flat-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, after changing absolute constants, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|T_n|_{V_\\lambda}\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n   &\\le \\exp[-c\\{\\Lambda_n(k)+\\log f_\\beta\\}],\n       \\label{rec:combined-tail-norm}\\\\\n W_n(\\lambda)&\\le e^{-c_1\\Lambda_n(k)}D_\\lambda^{-c_2}\n       \\label{rec:combined-dimension-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for absolute $c_1,c_2>0$.",
      "explanation": "Level contraction, tail-dimension contraction, a tuple density moment and a flat tuple trace bound are obtained from harmonic restriction.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "rec:sec-harmonic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T_n$ be one sweep on $n=2^d$ positions",

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-rec-main-harmonic-cycle"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rec-removal-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "For the component law above, $a$ is a mixture of $t+\\operatorname{Bin}(k-2t,1/2)$. In particular $$\\label{rec:split-tail}\n \\Pr(|a-k/2|\\ge z)\\le2e^{-2z^2/k},\\qquad\n \\mathbb Ee^{u(a-b)^2/k}\\le1+Cu\\quad(0\\le u\\le1/4).$$ For every fixed $\\eta>0$ and integer $l\\ge1$, $$\\label{rec:loss-binomial-general}\n \\mathbb E\\!\\left[\\mathbf1_{\\{m-a-r\\ge\\eta m\\}}\n                   {a-r\\choose l}\\right]\n       \\le(C_\\eta\\sqrt{k/n})^l,$$ and the same statement holds in the other half. Thus, with $\\alpha=1/8$ and $$L_1=(a-r)\\mathbf1_{\\{a\\le3m/4,\\ r<\\alpha m\\}},\\qquad\n L_2=(b-s)\\mathbf1_{\\{b\\le3m/4,\\ s<\\alpha m\\}},$$ we have $\\mathbb E{L_i\\choose l}\\le(C\\sqrt{k/n})^l$ and $$\\label{rec:loss-mgf}\n \\mathbb Ee^{u\\log(en/k)L_i}\\le1+Cu\n \\quad(0\\le u\\le u_0)$$ for an absolute $u_0>0$. A second useful form, with $\\alpha=1/16$, is $$\\label{rec:loss-tail}\n \\Pr\\big((a-r)\\mathbf1_{\\{r<\\alpha m\\}}\\ge x\\big)\n       \\le C e^{-cx\\log(en/k)}\\qquad(x\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge1}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-removal-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "For the component law above, $a$ is a mixture of $t+\\operatorname{Bin}(k-2t,1/2)$. Thus, with $\\alpha=1/8$ and $$L_1=(a-r)\\mathbf1_{\\{a\\le3m/4,\\ r<\\alpha m\\}},\\qquad\n L_2=(b-s)\\mathbf1_{\\{b\\le3m/4,\\ s<\\alpha m\\}},$$ we have $\\mathbb E{L_i\\choose l}\\le(C\\sqrt{k/n})^l$ and $$\\label{rec:loss-mgf}\n \\mathbb Ee^{u\\log(en/k)L_i}\\le1+Cu\n \\quad(0\\le u\\le u_0)$$ for an absolute $u_0>0$. A second useful form, with $\\alpha=1/16$, is $$\\label{rec:loss-tail}\n \\Pr\\big((a-r)\\mathbf1_{\\{r<\\alpha m\\}}\\ge x\\big)\n       \\le C e^{-cx\\log(en/k)}\\qquad(x\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge1}).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the component law above, $a$ is a mixture of $t+\\operatorname{Bin}(k-2t,1/2)$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **ordered injections and uniform measure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $0\le k\le N$, let $\mathcal X_{N,k}$ be the set of ordered lists of $k$ distinct positions, let $(N)_k=N!/(N-k)!$, and let $u_{N,k}$ be its uniform probability measure.
- **routing density** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $x,y\in\mathcal X_{N,k}$ define $R_x(y)=(N)_k\Pr_{K_N}(x\mapsto y)$.
- **sweep operator** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $T_N$ be the average action of one sweep.
- **reflected sweep kernel** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Thus $K_N=T_N^*T_N$ is the positive operator represented by a sweep followed by its reflection, using fresh independent switches.
- **mixing time** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)$ as the least number of physical shuffles at which this distance is at most $1/4$; by translation the distance is independent of the starting permutation.
- **level and tail diagram** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Write $k=n-\lambda_1$ and $\bar\lambda=(\lambda_2,\lambda_3,\ldots)$.
- **Casimir scale** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Put $k=N-\lambda_1$, $\beta=(\lambda_2,\lambda_3,\ldots)$, and $h_N(k)=k\log(eN/k)$, with $h_N(0)=0$.
- **unnormalized traces** ([source](#pm-h-7)): All matrix traces and Schatten norms are unnormalized.
- **harmonic function on injections** ([source](#n:coherent-contact-section)): A function $f:X\to\mathbb C$ is called harmonic if, for each slot, its sum over that slot is zero when the other slots are fixed.
- **box index** ([source](#rec:sec-cutoff)): For a box $x$ let $a(x)=\min(\operatorname{row}(x),\operatorname{column}(x))$, with indices beginning at one.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-density-excess-exponent | excess exponent in the density moment | = | $1/1000$ | absolute constant |
| q-support-tv-lower-bound | total variation distance after t physical shuffles | ≥ | $1-2^{tn/2}/n!$ | all integer t at least zero |
| q-butterfly-shared-switches | shared switches among h routed paths | ≤ | $h\log_2h/2$ | all h |
| q-sweep-norm-dimension-power | operator norm of one sweep on an irreducible | ≤ | $D_\lambda^{-c}$ | nontrivial irreducibles with nonzero sweep matrix |
| q-contact-sharp-bound | squared sweep norm on level k | ≤ | $\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{C_*dk}{n}\right)^{k/2}\right\}$ | 1 at most k below n |
| q-reciprocal-cycle-bound | extremal mean reciprocal cycle length | ≤ | $\sqrt{2/M}$ | all heights |
| q-dense-budget-full | dense budget function at full density | < | $1.958069$ | density equal to one |
| q-companion-shuffle-count | physical shuffles in the companion explicit bound | = | $1600d$ | d tending to infinity |
| q-cycle-density-norm | density norm at the Casimir scale | ≤ | $e^{C h_n(k)}$ | all starting tuples |
| q-core-power-norm | sweep norm on a surviving nontrivial type | ≤ | $2^{-cF_\lambda}$ | surviving nontrivial types |
| q-midpoint-dimension-cutoff | midpoint product-dimension cutoff | = | $0.50003F_\lambda$ | surviving nontrivial types |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-annihilation** [claim](#stmt-lem-annihilation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annihilation): Fair transposition in the sign average; Young rule for permutation modules. -> annihilated shapes
- **step-finite-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-finitegap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finitegap): Equality case of a product of projections forces invariance under cube-edge transpositions, which generate the symmetric group. -> strict norm gap at fixed size
- **step-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support): Counting reachable permutations from fair coin strings. -> lower bound on mixing time
- **step-schatten** [claim](#stmt-lem-schatten) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-schatten): Schatten Holder inequality. -> power bound for nonnormal sweeps
- **step-truncation** [claim](#stmt-net-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-net-truncation): Schur test for the small part and Hilbert-Schmidt counting over orthogonal copies. -> operator norm bound from multiplicity
- **step-butterfly-contacts** [claim](#stmt-n-butterfly-contacts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-butterfly-contacts): Induction on the last edited coordinate with a binary entropy inequality. -> contact bound
- **step-cycle-factorial** [claim](#stmt-fac-cycle-factorial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fac-cycle-factorial): Exploration of closing paths, conditional closure weights and contact accounting. -> factorial moments of cycles
- **step-density-moments** [claim](#stmt-fac-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fac-density): Expand the density recursively, bound cycle exponential moments by slabs and the tower property. -> density moments
- **step-main-factorial** [claim](#stmt-fac-main) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Truncate the density, use multiplicity of irreducibles, then Plancherel. -> representation contraction and mixing
- **step-high-tail** [claim](#stmt-net-hightail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-net-hightail): Fixed certificates of short cycles with conditional closing probabilities, banded tower argument. -> cycle moment generating bounds
- **step-dense-truncation** [claim](#stmt-net-densetruncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-net-densetruncation): Reciprocal cycle lengths, Hoeffding for node sums, union over low heights. -> dense tail bound
- **step-certificate-main** [claim](#stmt-net-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-net-main): Case analysis on level and dimension using truncation with sparse and dense cutoffs. -> dimension-power contraction
- **step-strong-density** [claim](#stmt-n-strong-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-strong-density): Encode cycles by omitted switch bits, ideal-node recursion, band summation. -> strong density bound
- **step-strong-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-n-strong-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-strong-smoothing): Interpolate between Lebesgue norms using self-adjointness of the kernel. -> pointwise smoothing
- **step-strong-tail** [claim](#stmt-n-strong-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-strong-tail): Fourier block on the label fibers, Schur orthogonality and the Schur test. -> lower-diagram dimension factor
- **step-contact** [claim](#stmt-n-coherent-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-coherent-contact): Alternating sum over retained coordinates, isolated vertex cancellation, weighted forest count. -> level contraction uniform in k
- **step-sparse-consequence** [claim](#stmt-n-coherent-sparse-consequence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-coherent-sparse-consequence): Combine sparse contact bound with the tail-dimension bound. -> dimension power in the sparse regime
- **step-casimir-fixed-level** [claim](#stmt-cas-fixed-level) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cas-fixed-level): Zero sums in slots and forest counting of interaction graphs. -> fixed-level contraction
- **step-casimir-main** [claim](#stmt-cas-main) / [proof](#pm-h-33): Casimir induction with random splits, Hausdorff-Young amplification, Plancherel. -> density, contraction and trace moment
- **step-harmonic-main** [claim](#stmt-rec-main-harmonic-cycle) / [proof](#pm-h-36): Harmonic slot removal and three level inductions. -> level and tail contraction
- **step-adaptive-main** [claim](#stmt-rec-adaptive-main) / [proof](#pm-h-43): Adaptive alphabets with potential amortization over the recursion tree. -> fourth trace moment
- **step-core-power** [claim](#stmt-rec-core-power-main) / [proof](#pm-h-49): Split the sweep at the midpoint and close the norm induction with a polynomial gap. -> dimension-power norm bound
- **step-midpoint** [claim](#stmt-rec-cutoff-midpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-cutoff-midpoint): Expand cut recursion with actual traces and a stopping diagnostic. -> midpoint overlap bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-butterfly-contact-bound**: For any collection of h distinct routed paths through a butterfly that updates each coordinate once, the total number of shared switches is at most h times the base-two logarithm of h, divided by two, with the value zero when h is zero.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-repeated-coordinate-layers**: Apply the shared-switch bound to a network in which some coordinate is updated more than once, or to a network that is not a butterfly. -> Two paths may then meet at several switches, so the total number of shared switches is no longer controlled by the logarithmic bound.
- **anti-contact-count-confusion**: Read the shared-switch bound as a bound on endpoint-counted contacts, or the reverse. -> The two quantities differ by a factor of two, so the bound is misapplied by that factor.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The lemma is a finite deterministic statement about explicit routed paths in a butterfly: the number of shared switches of a given finite set of paths is computed by a finite procedure and compared with the stated bound. The harness decides it only on listed small instances (PARTIAL coverage), not for all h and d.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin holds one line of integers: d, then h pairs x y (input and output position as d-bit integers, most significant bit is coordinate 1)
# PRECONDITIONS: d >= 1 integer, 0 <= x,y < 2^d, tokens whitespace separated
# OUTPUTS: the integer number of unordered path pairs sharing a switch (sum over switches of C(m,2)), or INVALID if two paths coincide in position at some stage boundary
# TIME: O(h * d^2) per input
# SPACE: O(h * d)
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, no floating point
# FAILURE: INVALID printed when two paths collide at a boundary; malformed input raises an exception (nonzero exit)
import sys
from collections import defaultdict


def main():
    t = sys.stdin.read().split()
    d = int(t[0])
    vals = [int(v) for v in t[1:]]
    paths = [(vals[i], vals[i + 1]) for i in range(0, len(vals) - 1, 2)]
    for b in range(1, d + 2):
        seen = set()
        for (x, y) in paths:
            pos = tuple([(y >> (d - j)) & 1 for j in range(1, b)] + [(x >> (d - j)) & 1 for j in range(b, d + 1)])
            if pos in seen:
                print('INVALID')
                return
            seen.add(pos)
    count = defaultdict(int)
    for (x, y) in paths:
        for i in range(1, d + 1):
            key = (i, tuple((y >> (d - j)) & 1 for j in range(1, i)), tuple((x >> (d - j)) & 1 for j in range(i + 1, d + 1)))
            count[key] += 1
    total = 0
    for m in count.values():
        total += m * (m - 1) // 2
    print(total)


main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives no reference implementation, library interface or API contract, only proofs about a random process, so there is no software artefact to port. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The shuffle originates in the study of Faro-type card shuffling. A virtual deck whose size is a power of two can be randomised by a fixed number of coordinate sweeps, so a game or simulation can use the sweep structure as a cheap shuffler. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No command-line tool, checker or verification utility is presented; the paper's outputs are estimates, not tooling. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The sweep is an explicit randomised procedure: d coordinate layers of independent fair pair swaps, repeated an absolute number of times. This is a concrete construction whose output law is shown to approach uniform. It is a sampler, not an optimisation or search routine. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-dyadic-size-only** (hypothesis): Every estimate is stated for a deck whose size is a power of two, with positions given by binary words. No bound is claimed for other sizes. Blocked use: ['Applying the mixing or contraction bounds to a deck or array whose size is not a power of two', 'Padding a non-dyadic deck and quoting the bound without a separate argument']
- **L-fair-independent-switches** (hypothesis): The random process uses independent, unbiased pair swaps in every coordinate layer. The proofs rely on this independence, so biased, correlated or adversarial switches are outside the results. Blocked use: ['Applying the contraction bounds to hardware or software switches with correlated or biased coins', 'Reusing one coin for several pairs and still quoting the bound']
- **L-nonnormal-sweep-powers** (formal_scope): The forward sweep is generally nonnormal, so a power of the positive reflected kernel is not a power of the sweep. Passing from one to the other requires the Schatten Holder step stated in the paper. Blocked use: ['Replacing a power of the reflected kernel by the corresponding power of the forward sweep in a norm estimate', 'Squaring a single-sweep norm to bound several sweeps without the stated lemma']
- **L-annihilated-shapes** (edge_case): Shapes with too many rows are annihilated by a sweep, so no dimension-power contraction is meaningful there. The contraction theorems concern only types whose sweep matrix is nonzero. Blocked use: ['Quoting a dimension-power norm bound for a shape that the sweep annihilates as if it described decay', 'Counting annihilated shapes among the surviving types in a Fourier sum']
- **L-certificate-regime** (formal_scope): The certificate-network contraction is asserted only for sufficiently large dyadic size and for nontrivial irreducibles not annihilated by a pair layer, with an unspecified absolute exponent. Blocked use: ['Using the bound at small sizes or with a numeric exponent that the paper does not supply', 'Applying it to the trivial or annihilated representations']
- **L-too-few-shuffles** (counterexample_scope): Counting available random bits forces a lower bound on the number of physical shuffles of order twice the dimension. A single sweep or fewer shuffles than this cannot reach the target distance. Blocked use: ['Claiming near-uniformity after one sweep or after a sublogarithmic number of physical shuffles', 'Using the upper bound with fewer shuffles than the support obstruction allows']
- **L-no-explicit-constant** (unknown): The theorem gives an absolute number of sweeps without a value, and states no sharp mixing constant or cutoff profile. An explicit sweep count must be taken from elsewhere. Blocked use: ['Using the result to set a concrete number of sweeps in an implementation', 'Claiming a cutoff phenomenon or a sharp constant from this paper']
- **L-existence-certificates** (nonconstructive): The high-height cycle tail argument shows that a favorable certificate exists for each counted network before a union bound. It does not construct the certificate or give an algorithm to find one. Blocked use: ['Presenting the certificate argument as a procedure that outputs the favorable cycles', 'Conditioning the coin law on the chosen favorable cycles']
- **L-butterfly-single-update** (hypothesis): The shared-switch bound holds for a butterfly that updates each binary coordinate exactly once. The count of shared switches and the count including both path endpoints differ. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to a network that updates some coordinate more than once', 'Reading the shared-switch bound as the endpoint-counted contact bound or the reverse']
- **L-sparse-regime-saving** (formal_scope): The exponential sparse-level saving requires the level to be small relative to the size in the stated logarithmic sense. It says nothing in the dense regime. Blocked use: ['Using the sparse-level saving for a level comparable to the deck size', 'Dropping the logarithmic condition on the ratio of size to level']
- **L-crypto-security-absent** (unknown): The paper bounds total variation distance for truly fair coins and gives no keyed construction, no pseudorandomness assumption and no security reduction. Blocked use: ['Treating the bare coordinate sweep as an encryption primitive', 'Inferring computational indistinguishability from a total variation bound']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 238 (`lean/docs/238.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Optimal-order-mixing-of-the-Thorp-shuffle-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds](../../preprints/From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Conditional permutations in a revealed switching environment](../../preprints/Conditional-permutations-in-a-revealed-switching-environment-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Routing densities and representation contraction for Thorp sweeps](../../preprints/Routing-densities-and-representation-contraction-for-Thorp-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Row–column symmetry and contraction of coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Row-column-symmetry-and-contraction-of-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Random-subspace tests and trace smoothing for coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Random-subspace-tests-and-trace-smoothing-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Compatibility entropy and the spectrum of a Thorp sweep](../../preprints/Compatibility-entropy-and-the-spectrum-of-a-Thorp-sweep-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Conditional coordinate sweeps and analytic transfer](../../preprints/Conditional-coordinate-sweeps-and-analytic-transfer-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [A strict four-row permanent inequality and permutation moments](../../preprints/A-strict-four-row-permanent-inequality-and-permutation-moments-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. After $1600d$ complete shuffles, the full permutation law converges in total variation to uniform as $d\to\infty$, uniformly over initial decks. Together with the support lower bound of $2d-O(1)$, this gives mixing time $\Theta(d)=\Theta(\log(2^d))$.
>
> The linked supporting results include frame and conditional-list estimates, regular trace and spectral bounds, signed moments, and full-density $L^2$ control. They also bound reciprocal Specht-dimension sums and give the eight-block Fourier estimate used in the mixing argument.
>
> The formalized supporting result turns bounds on partial-permutation cosets into Fourier bounds. Partition a finite set into $b\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $f\ge0$ be a subprobability weight on its symmetric group whose left-coset masses for each block subgroup are at most $B$. For every irreducible unitary representation of dimension $D$ and every $u>0$, both the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm and squared operator norm of its Fourier transform are at most $bB\,C(u)D^{-1+(u+2)/b}$, where $C(u)$ is the stated symmetric-group degree constant.
>
> The paper's asymptotic conclusion about the product of two random permutations is outside this selected finite estimate.
>
> The formalized results give averaged conditional mixing for half-permutations in the revealed-path model and full-deck mixing for the Thorp shuffle. In particular, the full-deck total-variation distance tends to zero after $16040400d$ steps on $2^d$ cards, uniformly over deterministic initial decks. The conditional estimate is averaged over the actual outside-path law, rather than asserted for each individual environment. The formalization also includes the associated overlay, reset minorization, and two-color conditional constructions.
>
> The formalization covers nine routing and representation estimates for Thorp sweeps: adaptive operator and fourth-trace bounds, Casimir moments, dense truncation, sparse contact, harmonic-cycle contraction, smoothing, and three tail or sparse-saving estimates. They bound routing-density deviations and Fourier operators in dense and sparse regimes, including exponential savings in the level scale and representation dimension.
>
> The conditional high-height estimate requires a sufficiently large cutoff height $J$ that exceeds twice the number $s$ of coordinates in the fixed low block, so $J>2s$. These are the earlier nine statements associated with the paper; its changed later statements and six other result blocks are outside this scope.
>
> The formalization proves uniform representation bounds for one coordinate sweep of the Thorp shuffle. For each irreducible representation of dimension $D$, it chooses a weight between $D^{3/4}$ and $D$ and a Schatten exponent bounded by one absolute constant so that the weighted Schatten moment is at most one. The sweep's operator norm is consequently at most $D^{-c}$ for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The supporting row–column estimate bounds the total squared overlap over all multiplicity copies on an occupied board, with explicit dependence on the row and column representation dimensions, the global dimension, the number of missing cells, and the number of rows or columns containing the global Young diagram. The paper's full physical-mixing conclusion is outside these selected representation estimates.
>
> The formalization proves a uniform trace-smoothing estimate for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. One absolute sweep parameter works for every $d\ge1$: the regular trace is at most $1+(2^d)^{-10}$, and the full permutation law after the corresponding fixed number of sweeps is within $\tfrac12(2^d)^{-5}$ of uniform in total variation, for every initial deck. Thus the selected upper bound uses $O(d)$ physical shuffles.
>
> The formalized result is the weighted compatibility theorem for balanced $A\times D$ rectangles, with $n=AD$ and $\sqrt n/2\le A,D\le2\sqrt n$. For arbitrary nonnegative weights on the row and column permutation groups, the normalized compatibility average is bounded by $\exp(C_0n^{54/100})$ times the product of the marginal $L^{1/\theta}$ factors, where $\theta=1-L/\log\sqrt n>0$ and the constants are absolute. Compatibility means injectivity in every original column. The bounded regular-moment and other moment/rank conclusions are not included.
>
> The formalization gives signed representation estimates for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle. For every signed occurrence of a Young diagram of size $2^d$, it bounds the logarithm of a fixed-order weighted sweep moment by a small multiple of the signed partition entropy plus an explicit remainder-size budget. The moment order is uniform over the diagrams and decompositions after the two small coefficients are fixed.
>
> The linked supporting angle bound controls the overlap of row, column, and global signed-type projections on an occupied rectangle, with explicit entropy, dimension, and missing-cell factors. These selected estimates support the paper's full-density argument; the full $L^2$ and total-variation mixing conclusion is outside them.
>
> The formalized binary-sweep theorem gives a universal power contraction in every irreducible representation: for sufficiently large $d$, the averaged sweep on $2^d$ slots has operator norm at most $D^{-g}$ in representation dimension $D$, for some $g>0$. The sign expectation is zero, and a fixed number of independent sweeps approaches uniform in total variation from every initial deck. A separate conditional moment estimate covers allowed power-of-two grids and disjoint coordinate-respecting trajectories, with its stated feasibility premise. Physical-shuffle law identification is not included.
>
> The formalization proves the paper's strict four-row permanent inequality. There are constants $4/3<p<2$ and $\varepsilon>0$ such that every probability law on $S_4$ within total-variation distance $\varepsilon$ of uniform, and with exactly uniform coordinate marginals, satisfies the permanent bound by the product of the four $L^p$ row norms for every nonnegative matrix. The exponent and neighborhood are uniform over those laws. The Thorp mixing consequence is outside this selected statement.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace CubeShuffle.Specht

open scoped BigOperators Classical

abbrev Cell (μ : YoungDiagram) := {x : ℕ × ℕ // x ∈ μ.cells}
def row {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : ℕ := x.1.1
def col {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : ℕ := x.1.2

lemma row_lt_colLen {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : row x < μ.colLen (col x) :=
  YoungDiagram.mem_iff_lt_colLen.mp x.2
noncomputable def rowIndex {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : Fin (μ.colLen 0) :=
  ⟨row x, (row_lt_colLen x).trans_le (μ.colLen_anti 0 (col x) (Nat.zero_le _))⟩

abbrev Tabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) :=
  {f : Cell μ → Fin (μ.colLen 0) // ∃ p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ), f = rowIndex ∘ p}

noncomputable instance (μ : YoungDiagram) : Fintype (Tabloid μ) := Fintype.ofFinite _

noncomputable def baseTabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) : Tabloid μ := ⟨rowIndex, 1, rfl⟩

noncomputable def tabloidAct {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    Equiv.Perm (Tabloid μ) where
  toFun f := ⟨fun x => f.1 (p⁻¹ x), by
    obtain ⟨q,hq⟩ := f.2
    refine ⟨q*p⁻¹, ?_⟩
    funext x
    simp only [hq,Function.comp_apply,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]⟩
  invFun f := ⟨fun x => f.1 (p x), by
    obtain ⟨q,hq⟩ := f.2
    refine ⟨q*p, ?_⟩
    funext x
    simp only [hq,Function.comp_apply,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]⟩
  left_inv f := by apply Subtype.ext; funext x; simp
  right_inv f := by apply Subtype.ext; funext x; simp

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_apply {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))
    (f : Tabloid μ) (x : Cell μ) : (tabloidAct p f).1 x = f.1 (p⁻¹ x) := rfl

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_one (μ : YoungDiagram) : tabloidAct (1 : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) = 1 := by
  ext f x
  rfl

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_mul {μ : YoungDiagram} (p q : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    tabloidAct (p*q) = tabloidAct p * tabloidAct q := by
  ext f x
  simp only [tabloidAct_apply, mul_inv_rev,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_inv {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    tabloidAct p⁻¹ = (tabloidAct p)⁻¹ := by
  ext f x
  simp [tabloidAct,Equiv.Perm.inv_def]

noncomputable def tabloidRep (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (Tabloid μ → ℂ) where
  toFun p :=
    { toFun := fun v f => v (tabloidAct p⁻¹ f)
      map_add' := by intros; rfl
      map_smul' := by intros; rfl }
  map_one' := by ext v f; simp
  map_mul' p q := by
    ext v f
    simp only [mul_inv_rev,tabloidAct_mul,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]
    rfl

@[simp] lemma tabloidRep_apply {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))
    (v : Tabloid μ → ℂ) (f : Tabloid μ) : tabloidRep μ p v f = v (tabloidAct p⁻¹ f) := rfl

noncomputable def delta {μ : YoungDiagram} (f : Tabloid μ) : Tabloid μ → ℂ :=
  fun g => if g=f then 1 else 0

noncomputable def colGroup (μ : YoungDiagram) : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) where
  carrier := {p | ∀ x, col (p x) = col x}
  one_mem' := by intro x; rfl
  mul_mem' := by intro p q hp hq x; exact (hp (q x)).trans (hq x)
  inv_mem' := by intro p hp x; simpa using (hp (p⁻¹ x)).symm

noncomputable def permSign {α : Type*} [Fintype α] [DecidableEq α] (p : Equiv.Perm α) : ℂ :=
  ((Equiv.Perm.sign p : ℤ) : ℂ)
section Alternator
variable {α V : Type*} [Fintype α] [DecidableEq α] [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V]

noncomputable def alternator (C : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm α))
    (ρ : Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm α) V) : Module.End ℂ V := by
  classical
  exact ∑ c : C, permSign (c : Equiv.Perm α) • ρ c

end Alternator

noncomputable def polytabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) : Tabloid μ → ℂ :=
  alternator (colGroup μ) (tabloidRep μ) (delta (baseTabloid μ))

noncomputable def space (μ : YoungDiagram) : Submodule ℂ (Tabloid μ → ℂ) :=
  Submodule.span ℂ (Set.range (fun p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ) => tabloidRep μ p (polytabloid μ)))

lemma orbit_mem_space (μ : YoungDiagram) (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    tabloidRep μ p (polytabloid μ) ∈ space μ := Submodule.subset_span ⟨p,rfl⟩

lemma action_mem_space (μ : YoungDiagram) (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))
    (v : Tabloid μ → ℂ) (hv : v ∈ space μ) : tabloidRep μ p v ∈ space μ := by
  induction hv using Submodule.span_induction with
  | mem v hv =>
    obtain ⟨q,rfl⟩ := hv
    change ((tabloidRep μ p)*(tabloidRep μ q)) (polytabloid μ) ∈ space μ
    rw [←map_mul]
    exact orbit_mem_space μ (p*q)
  | zero => simp
  | add v w hv hw ihv ihw => simpa using (space μ).add_mem ihv ihw
  | smul a v hv ih => simpa using (space μ).smul_mem a ih

noncomputable def subrepresentation (μ : YoungDiagram) : Subrepresentation (tabloidRep μ) :=
  ⟨space μ, fun p v hv => action_mem_space μ p v hv⟩

noncomputable def representation (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (space μ) :=
  (subrepresentation μ).toRepresentation

end CubeShuffle.Specht

namespace CubeShuffle.PermutationHilbert

open scoped BigOperators ComplexConjugate Classical
open Complex

variable {X : Type*} [Fintype X]

abbrev H (X : Type*) [Fintype X] := EuclideanSpace ℂ X

end CubeShuffle.PermutationHilbert

namespace CubeShuffle.Specht

open scoped BigOperators Classical

noncomputable def hilbertEquiv (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    (Tabloid μ → ℂ) ≃ₗ[ℂ] PermutationHilbert.H (Tabloid μ) :=
  (WithLp.linearEquiv 2 ℂ (Tabloid μ → ℂ)).symm

noncomputable def hilbertSpace (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Submodule ℂ (PermutationHilbert.H (Tabloid μ)) := (space μ).map (hilbertEquiv μ).toLinearMap

noncomputable def spaceHilbertEquiv (μ : YoungDiagram) : space μ ≃ₗ[ℂ] hilbertSpace μ :=
  (hilbertEquiv μ).submoduleMap (space μ)

noncomputable def unitaryRepresentation (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (hilbertSpace μ) :=
  ((spaceHilbertEquiv μ).conjAlgEquiv ℂ).toMonoidHom.comp (representation μ)

variable {α ι : Type*} [Fintype α] [Fintype ι]

noncomputable def relabelledUnitary (μ : YoungDiagram) (e : α ≃ Cell μ) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm α) (hilbertSpace μ) :=
  (unitaryRepresentation μ).comp e.permCongrHom.toMonoidHom

end CubeShuffle.Specht

namespace CubeShuffle

open scoped BigOperators
open Filter

abbrev Card (d : ℕ) := Fin d → Bool

def Coins : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Unit
  | d + 1 => Card d → Bool

instance (d : ℕ) : Fintype (Coins d) := by
  cases d <;> dsimp [Coins] <;> infer_instance

instance (d : ℕ) : Nonempty (Coins d) := by
  cases d <;> dsimp [Coins] <;> infer_instance

def switchFun {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) (x : Card (d + 1)) : Card (d + 1) :=
  Fin.cons (Bool.xor (x 0) (ξ (Fin.tail x))) (Fin.tail x)

theorem switchFun_involutive {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) :
    Function.Involutive (switchFun ξ) := by
  intro x
  funext i
  refine Fin.cases ?_ (fun j => ?_) i
  · simp only [switchFun, Fin.cons_zero, Fin.tail_cons]
    cases x 0 <;> cases ξ (Fin.tail x) <;> rfl
  · simp [switchFun, Fin.tail]

def pairSwitch {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) : Equiv.Perm (Card (d + 1)) :=
  { toFun := switchFun ξ
    invFun := switchFun ξ
    left_inv := switchFun_involutive ξ
    right_inv := switchFun_involutive ξ }

def rotate (d : ℕ) : Equiv.Perm (Card d) where
  toFun x i := x (finRotate d i)
  invFun x i := x ((finRotate d).symm i)
  left_inv x := by funext i; simp only [Equiv.apply_symm_apply]
  right_inv x := by funext i; simp only [Equiv.symm_apply_apply]

def step : (d : ℕ) → Coins d → Equiv.Perm (Card d)
  | 0, _ => 1
  | d + 1, ξ => rotate (d + 1) * pairSwitch ξ

def run (d : ℕ) : (t : ℕ) → (Fin t → Coins d) → Equiv.Perm (Card d)
  | 0, _ => 1
  | t + 1, ω => step d (ω (Fin.last t)) * run d t (fun i => ω i.castSucc)

lemma card_positions (d : ℕ) : Fintype.card (Card d) = 2 ^ d := by
  simp [Card]

end CubeShuffle

namespace CubeShuffle.Specht
open scoped BigOperators Classical

@[simp] lemma card_cell (μ : YoungDiagram) : Fintype.card (Cell μ) = μ.card := by
  simp [Cell, YoungDiagram.card]

abbrev Shapes (N : ℕ) := {μ : YoungDiagram // μ.card = N}

noncomputable def labels {N : ℕ} (μ : Shapes N) : Cell μ.1 ≃ Fin N :=
  (Fintype.equivFin (Cell μ.1)).trans (finCongr ((card_cell μ.1).trans μ.2))

noncomputable def cardLabels (d : ℕ) (μ : Shapes (2^d)) : Card d ≃ Cell μ.1 :=
  ((Fintype.equivFin (Card d)).trans (finCongr (card_positions d))).trans (labels μ).symm

noncomputable def cardRepresentation (d : ℕ) (μ : Shapes (2^d)) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Card d)) (hilbertSpace μ.1) :=
  relabelledUnitary μ.1 (cardLabels d μ)

end CubeShuffle.Specht

namespace CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite
open scoped BigOperators ComplexConjugate Classical
variable {G : Type*} [Group G] [Fintype G]
variable {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V] [FiniteDimensional ℂ V]

noncomputable def continuousRepresentation (ρ : Representation ℂ G V) : G →* (V →L[ℂ] V) where
  toFun g := (ρ g).toContinuousLinearMap
  map_one' := by ext v; simp
  map_mul' g h := by ext v; simp

end CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite

run_cmd Lean.modifyEnv fun env => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState env {}

namespace CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite
open scoped BigOperators Classical
variable {G : Type*} [Group G] [Fintype G]
variable {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V] [FiniteDimensional ℂ V]
variable {Ω Ξ : Type*} [Fintype Ω] [Fintype Ξ]

noncomputable def sampleOperator (ρ : Representation ℂ G V) (P : Ω → G) : V →L[ℂ] V :=
  (Fintype.card Ω:ℂ)⁻¹ • ∑ ω,continuousRepresentation ρ (P ω)

end CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Classical

namespace CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep
open Specht UnitaryFinite

def dimension (μ : YoungDiagram) : ℝ := Module.finrank ℂ (space μ)
def fourierSweep (d : ℕ) (μ : Shapes (2^d)) :
    hilbertSpace μ.1 →L[ℂ] hilbertSpace μ.1 :=
  sampleOperator (V := hilbertSpace μ.1) (cardRepresentation d μ) (run d d)
def schattenMoment {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V]
    [FiniteDimensional ℂ V] (A : V →L[ℂ] V) (p : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i : Fin (Module.finrank ℂ V), (A.toLinearMap.singularValues i) ^ p

def schattenNorm {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V]
    [FiniteDimensional ℂ V] (A : V →L[ℂ] V) (p : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  schattenMoment A p ^ (1 / p)

end CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep

end

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Classical

namespace CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep
open Specht UnitaryFinite

theorem weighted_sweep_moments :
    ∃ (W : YoungDiagram → ℝ) (pStar : ℝ) (p : ℕ → ℝ),
      (∀ μ : YoungDiagram, 0 < W μ) ∧
      2 ≤ pStar ∧
      (∀ d : ℕ, 1 ≤ d → 2 ≤ p d ∧ p d ≤ pStar) ∧
      ∀ d : ℕ, 1 ≤ d → ∀ μ : Shapes (2 ^ d),
        dimension μ.1 ^ (3 / 4 : ℝ) ≤ W μ.1 ∧
        W μ.1 ≤ dimension μ.1 ∧
        W μ.1 *
          schattenNorm (V := hilbertSpace μ.1) (fourierSweep d μ) (p d) ^ (p d) ≤ 1 ∧
        ‖fourierSweep d μ‖ ≤ dimension μ.1 ^ (-3 / (4 * pStar)) := by
  sorry

end CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep

end

end OAI
```
